Basic support of [JSON Web Keys](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7517.html) (JWK).\n2: //!\n3: //! The functionality defined in this module allows converting between\n4: //! the [generic JWK format](JsonWebKey) and key presentation specific for the crypto backend.\n5: //! [`JsonWebKey`]s can be (de)serialized using [`serde`] infrastructure, and can be used\n6: //! to compute key thumbprint as per [RFC 7638].\n7: //!\n8: //! [`serde`]: https://crates.io/crates/serde\n9: //! [RFC 7638]: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7638\n10: //!\n11: //! # Examples\n12: //!\n13: //! ```\n14: //! use jwt_compact::{alg::Hs256Key, jwk::JsonWebKey};\n15: //! use sha2::Sha256;\n16: //!\n17: //! # fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n18: //! // Load a key from the JWK presentation.\n19: //! let json_str = r#\"\n20: //!     { \"kty\": \"oct\", \"k\": \"t-bdv41MJXExXnpquHBuDn7n1YGyX7gLQchVHAoNu50\" }\n21: //! \"#;\n22: //! let jwk: JsonWebKey<'_> = serde_json::from_str(json_str)?;\n23: //! let key = Hs256Key::try_from(&jwk)?;\n24: //!\n25: //! // Convert `key` back to JWK.\n26: //! let jwk_from_key = JsonWebKey::from(&key);\n27: //! assert_eq!(jwk_from_key, jwk);\n28: //! println!(\"{}\", serde_json::to_string(&jwk)?);\n29: //!\n30: //! // Compute the key thumbprint.\n31: //! let thumbprint = jwk_from_key.thumbprint::<Sha256>();\n32: //! # Ok(())\n33: //! # }\n34: //! ```\n35: \n36: use serde::{Deserialize, Deserializer, Serialize, Serializer};\n37: use sha2::digest::{Digest, Output};\n38: \n39: use core::fmt;\n40: \n41: use crate::{\n42:     alg::SecretBytes,\n43:     alloc::{Cow, String, ToString, Vec},\n44: };\n45: \n46: /// Type of a [`JsonWebKey`].\n47: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\n48: #[non_exhaustive]\n49: pub enum KeyType {\n50:     /// Public or private RSA key. Corresponds to the `RSA` value of the `kty` field for JWKs.\n51:     Rsa,\n52:     /// Public or private key in an ECDSA crypto system. Corresponds to the `EC` value\n53:     /// of the `kty` field for JWKs.\n54:     EllipticCurve,\n55:     /// Symmetric key. Corresponds to the `oct` value of the `kty` field for JWKs.\n56:     Symmetric,\n57:     /// Generic asymmetric keypair. Corresponds to the `OKP` value of the `kty` field for JWKs.\n58:     KeyPair,\n59: }\n60: \n61: impl fmt::Display for KeyType {\n62:     fn fmt(&self, formatter: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n63:         formatter.write_str(match self {\n64:             Self::Rsa => \"RSA\",\n65:             Self::EllipticCurve => \"EC\",\n66:             Self::Symmetric => \"oct\",\n67:             Self::KeyPair => \"OKP\",\n68:         })\n69:     }\n70: }\n71: \n72: /// Errors that can occur when transforming a [`JsonWebKey`] into the presentation specific for\n73: /// a crypto backend, using the [`TryFrom`] trait.\n74: #[derive(Debug)]\n75: #[non_exhaustive]\n76: pub enum JwkError {\n77:     /// Required field is absent from JWK.\n78:     NoField(String),\n79:     /// Key       actual: usize,\n103:     },\n104:     /// Signing and verifying keys do not match.\n105:     MismatchedKeys,\n106:     /// Custom error specific to a crypto backend.\n107:     Custom(anyhow::Error),\n108: }\n109: \n110: impl fmt::Display for JwkError {\n111:     fn fmt(&self, formatter: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n112:         match self {\n113:             Self::UnexpectedKeyType { expected, actual } => {\n114:                 write!(\n115:                     formatter,\n116:                     \"unexpected key type: {actual} (expected {expected})\"\n117:                 )\n118:             }\n119:             Self::NoField(field) => write!(formatter, \"field `{field}` is absent from JWK\"),\n120:             Self::UnexpectedValue {\n121:                 field,\n122:                 expected,\n123:                 actual,\n124:             } => {\n125:                 write!(\n126:                     formatter,\n127: formatter.write_str(\"private and public keys encoded in JWK do not match\")\n142:             }\n143:             Self::Custom(err) => fmt::Display::fmt(err, formatter),\n144:         }\n145:     }\n146: }\n147: \n148: #[cfg(feature = \"std\")]\n149: impl std::error::Error for JwkError {\n150:     fn source(&self) -> Option<&(dyn std::error::Error + 'static)> {\n151:         match self {\n152:             Self::Custom(err) => Some(err.as_ref()),\n153:             _ => None,\n154:         }\n155:     }\n156: }\n157: \n158: impl JwkError {\n159:     /// Creates a `Custom` error variant.\n160:     pub fn custom(err: impl Into<anyhow::Error>) -> Self {\n161:         Self::Custom(err.into())\n162:     }\n163: \n164:     pub(crate) fn key_type(jwk: &JsonWebKey<'_>, expected: KeyType) -> Self {\n165:         let actual = jwk.key_type();\n166:         debug_assert_ne!(actual, expected);\n167:         Self::UnexpectedKeyType { actual, expected }\n168:     }\n169: }\n170: \n171: impl Serialize for SecretBytes<'_> {\n172:     fn serialize<S: Serializer>(&self, serializer: S) -> Result<S::Ok, S::Error> {\n173:         base64url::serialize(self.as_ref(), serializer)\n174:     }\n175: }\n176: \n177: impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for SecretBytes<'_> {\n178:     fn deserialize<D: Deserializer<'de>>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Self, D::Error> {\n179:         base64url::deserialize(deserializer).map(SecretBytes::new)\n180:     }\n181: }\n182: \n183: /// Basic [JWK] functionality: (de)serialization and creating thumbprints.\n184: ///\n185: /// See [RFC 7518] for the details about the fields for various key types.\n186: ///\n187: /// [`Self::thumbprint()`] and the [`Display`](fmt::Display) implementation\n188: /// allow to get the overall presentation of the key. The latter returns JSON serialization\n189: /// of the key with fields ordered alphabetically. That is, this output for verifying keys\n190: /// can be used to compute key thumbprints.\n191: ///\n192: /// # Serialization\n193: ///\n194: /// For human-readable formats (e.g., JSON, TOML, YAML), byte fields in `JsonWebKey`\n195: /// and embedded types ([`SecretBytes`], [`RsaPrivateParts`], [`RsaPrimeFactor`]) will be\n196: /// serialized in base64-url encoding with no padding, as per the JWK spec.\n197: /// For other formats (e.g., CBOR), byte fields will be serialized as byte sequences.\n198: ///\n199: /// Because of [the limitations](https://github.com/pyfisch/cbor/issues/3)\n200: /// of the CBOR support in `serde`, a `JsonWebKey` serialized in CBOR is **not** compliant\n201: /// with the [CBOR Object Signing and Encryption spec][COSE] (COSE). It can still be a good\n202: /// way to decrease the serialized key size.\n203: ///\n204: /// # Conversions\n205: ///\n206: /// A JWK can be obtained from signing and verifying keys defined in the [`alg`](crate::alg)\n207: /// module via [`From`] / [`Into`] traits. Conversion from a JWK to a specific key is fallible\n208: /// and can be performed via [`TryFrom`] with [`JwkError`] as an error\n209: /// type.\n210: ///\n211: /// As a part of conversion for asymmetric signing keys, it is checked whether\n212: /// the signing and verifying parts of the JWK match; [`JwkError::MismatchedKeys`] is returned\n213: /// otherwise. This check is **not** performed for verifying keys even if the necessary data\n214: /// is present in the provided JWK.\n215: ///\n216: /// ⚠ **Warning.** Conversions for private RSA keys are not fully compliant with [RFC 7518].\n217: /// See the docs for the relevant `impl`s for more details.\n218: ///\n219: /// [RFC 7518]: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7518#section-6\n220: /// [JWK]: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7517.html\n221: /// [COSE]: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc8152\n222: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n223: #[serde(tag = \"kty\")]\n224: #[non_exhaustive]\n225: pub enum JsonWebKey<'a> {\n226:     /// Public or private RSA key. Has `kty` field set to `RSA`.\n227:     #[serde(rename = \"RSA\")]\n228:     Rsa {\n229:         /// Key modulus (`n`).\n230:         #[serde(rename = \"n\", with = \"base64url\")]\n231:         modulus: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n232:         /// Public exponent (`e`).\n233:         #[serde(rename = \"e\", with = \"base64url\")]\n234:         public_exponent: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n235:         /// Private RSA parameters. Only present for private keys.\n236:         #[serde(flatten)]\n237:         private_parts: Option<RsaPrivateParts<'a>>,\n238:     },\n239:     /// Public or private key in an ECDSA crypto system. Has `kty` field set to `EC`.\n240:     #[serde(rename = \"EC\")]\n241:     EllipticCurve {\n242:         /// Curve name (`crv`), such as `secp256k1`.\n243:         #[serde(rename = \"crv\")]\n244:         curve: Cow<'a, str>,\n245:         /// `x` coordinate of the curve point.\n246:         #[serde(with = \"base64url\")]\n247:         x: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n248:         /// `y` coordinate of the curve point.\n249:         #[serde(with = \"base64url\")]\n250:         y: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n251:         /// Secret scalar (`d`); not present for public keys.\n252:         #[serde(rename = \"d\", default, skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n253:         secret: Option<SecretBytes<'a>>,\n254:     },\n255:     /// Generic symmetric key, e.g. for `HS256` algorithm. Has `kty` field set to `oct`.\n256:     #[serde(rename = \"oct\")]\n257:     Symmetric {\n258:         /// Bytes representing this key.\n259:         #[serde(rename = \"k\")]\n260:         secret: SecretBytes<'a>,\n261:     },\n262:     /// Generic asymmetric keypair. This key type is used e.g. for Ed25519 keys.\n263:     #[serde(rename = \"OKP\")]\n264:     KeyPair {\n265:         /// Curve name (`crv`), such as `Ed25519`.\n266:         #[serde(rename = \"crv\")]\n267:         curve: Cow<'a, str>,\n268:         /// Public key. For Ed25519, this is the standard 32-byte public key presentation\n269:         /// (`x` coordinate of a point on the curve + sign).\n270:         #[serde(with = \"base64url\")]\n271:         x: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n272:         /// Secret key (`d`). For Ed25519, this is the seed.\n273:         #[serde(rename = \"d\", default, skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n274:         secret: Option<SecretBytes<'a>>,\n275:     },\n276: }\n277: \n278: impl JsonWebKey<'_> {\n279:     /// Gets the type of this key.\n280:     pub fn key_type(&self) -> KeyType {\n281:         match self {\n282:             Self::Rsa { .. } => KeyType::Rsa,\n283:             Self::EllipticCurve { .. } => KeyType::EllipticCurve,\n284:             Self::Symmetric { .. } => KeyType::Symmetric,\n285:             Self::KeyPair { .. } => KeyType::KeyPair,\n286:         }\n287:     }\n288: \n289:     /// Returns `true` if this key can be used for signing (has [`SecretBytes`] fields).\n290:     pub fn is_signing_key(&self) -> bool {\n291:         match self {\n292:             Self::Rsa { private_parts, .. } => private_parts.is_some(),\n293:             Self::EllipticCurve { secret, .. } | Self::KeyPair { secret, .. } => secret.is_some(),\n294:             Self::Symmetric { .. } => true,\n295:         }\n296:     }\n297: \n298:     /// Returns a copy of this key with parts not necessary for signature verification removed.\n299:     #[must_use]\n300:     pub fn to_verifying_key(&self) -> Self {\n301:         match self {\n302:             Self::Rsa {\n303:                 modulus,\n304:                 public_exponent,\n305:                 ..\n306:             } => Self::Rsa {\n307:                 modulus: modulus.clone(),\n308:                 public_exponent: public_exponent.clone(),\n309:                 private_parts: None,\n310:             },\n311: \n312:             Self::EllipticCurve { curve, x, y, .. } => Self::EllipticCurve {\n313:                 curve: curve.clone(),\n314:                 x: x.clone(),\n315:                 y: [`JsonWebKey`]s can be (de)serialized using [`serde`] infrastructure, and can be used\n//! to compute key thumbprint as per [RFC 7638].\n//!\n//! [`serde`]: https://crates.io/crates/serde\n//! Basic support of [JSON Web Keys](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7517.html) (JWK).\n//!\n//! The functionality defined in this module allows converting between\n//! the [generic JWK format](JsonWebKey) and key presentation specific for the crypto backend.\n//! [`JsonWebKey`]s can be (de)serialized using [`serde`] infrastructure, and can be used\n//! to compute key thumbprint as per [RFC 7638].\n//!\n//! [`serde`]: https://crates.io/crates/serde\n//! [RFC 7638]: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7638\n//!\n//! # Examples\n//!\n//! ```\n//! use jwt_compact::{alg::Hs256Key, jwk::JsonWebKey};\n//! use sha2::Sha256;\n//!\n//! # fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n//! // Load a key from the JWK presentation.\n//! let json_str = r#\"\n//!     { \"kty\": \"oct\", \"k\": \"t-bdv41MJXExXnpquHBuDn7n1YGyX7gLQchVHAoNu50\" }\n//! \"#;\n//! let jwk: JsonWebKey<'_> = serde_json::from_str(json_str)?;\n//! let key = Hs256Key::try_from(&jwk)?;\n//!\n//! // Convert `key` back to JWK.\n//! let jwk_from_key = JsonWebKey::from(&key);\n//! assert_eq!(jwk_from_key, jwk);\n//! println!(\"{}\", serde_json::to_string(&jwk)?);\n//!\n//! // Compute the key thumbprint.\n//! let thumbprint = jwk_from_key.thumbprint::<Sha256>();\n//! # Ok(())\n//! # }\n//! ```\n\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Deserializer, Serialize, Serializer};\nuse sha2::digest::{Digest, Output};\n\nuse core::fmt;\n\nuse crate::{\n    alg::SecretBytes,\n    alloc::{Cow, String, ToString, Vec},\n};\n\n/// Type of a [`JsonWebKey`].\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\n#[non_exhaustive]\npub enum KeyType {\n    /// Public or private RSA key. Corresponds to the `RSA` value of the `kty` field for JWKs.\n    Rsa,\n    /// Public or private key in an ECDSA crypto system. Corresponds to the `EC` value\n    /// of the `kty` field for JWKs.\n    EllipticCurve,\n    /// Symmetric key. Corresponds to the `oct` value of the `kty` field for JWKs.\n    Symmetric,\n    /// Generic asymmetric keypair. Corresponds to the `OKP` value of the `kty` field for JWKs.\n    KeyPair,\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for KeyType {\n    fn fmt(&self, formatter: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        formatter.write_str(match self {\n            Self::Rsa => \"RSA\",\n            Self::EllipticCurve => \"EC\",\n            Self::Symmetric => \"oct\",\n            Self::KeyPair => \"OKP\",\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n/// Errors that can occur when transforming a [`JsonWebKey`] into the presentation specific for\n/// a crypto backend, using the [`TryFrom`] trait.\n#[derive(Debug)]\n#[non_exhaustive]\npub enum JwkError {\n    /// Required field is absent from JWK.\n    NoField(String),\n    /// Key type (the `kty` field) is not as expected.\n    UnexpectedKeyType {\n        /// Expected key type.\n        expected: KeyType,\n        /// Actual key type.\n        actual: KeyType,\n    },\n    /// JWK field has an unexpected value.\n    UnexpectedValue {\n        /// Field name.\n        field: String,\n        /// Expected value of the field.\n        expected: String,\n        /// Actual value of the field.\n        actual: String,\n    },\n    /// JWK field has an unexpected byte length.\n    UnexpectedLen {\n        /// Field name.\n        field: String,\n        /// Expected byte length of the field.\n        expected: usize,\n        /// Actual byte length of the field.\n        actual: usize,\n    },\n    /// Signing and verifying keys do not match.\n    MismatchedKeys,\n    /// Custom error specific to a crypto backend.\n    Custom(anyhow::Error),\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for JwkError {\n    fn fmt(&self, formatter: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            Self::UnexpectedKeyType { expected, actual } => {\n                write!(\n                    formatter,\n                    \"unexpected key type: {actual} (expected {expected})\"\n                )\n            }\n            Self::NoField(field) => write!(formatter, \"field `{field}` is absent from JWK\"),\n            Self::UnexpectedValue {\n                field,\n                expected,\n                actual,\n            } => {\n                write!(\n                    formatter,\n                    \"field `{field}` has unexpected value (expected: {expected}, got: {actual})\"\n                )\n            }\n            Self::UnexpectedLen {\n                field,\n                expected,\n                actual,\n            } => {\n                write!(\n                    formatter,\n                    \"field `{field}` has unexpected length (expected: {expected}, got: {actual})\"\n                )\n            }\n            Self::MismatchedKeys => {\n                formatter.write_str(\"private and public keys encoded in JWK do not match\")\n            }\n            Self::Custom(err) => fmt::Display::fmt(err, formatter),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"std\")]\nimpl std::error::Error for JwkError {\n    fn source(&self) -> Option<&(dyn std::error::Error + 'static)> {\n        match self {\n            Self::Custom(err) => Some(err.as_ref()),\n            _ => None,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl JwkError {\n    /// Creates a `Custom` error variant.\n    pub fn custom(err: impl Into<anyhow::Error>) -> Self {\n        Self::Custom(err.into())\n    }\n\n    pub(crate) fn key_type(jwk: &JsonWebKey<'_>, expected: KeyType) -> Self {\n        let actual = jwk.key_type();\n        debug_assert_ne!(actual, expected);\n        Self::UnexpectedKeyType { actual, expected }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Serialize for SecretBytes<'_> {\n    fn serialize<S: Serializer>(&self, serializer: S) -> Result<S::Ok, S::Error> {\n        base64url::serialize(self.as_ref(), serializer)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for SecretBytes<'_> {\n    fn deserialize<D: Deserializer<'de>>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Self, D::Error> {\n        base64url::deserialize(deserializer).map(SecretBytes::new)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Basic [JWK] functionality: (de)serialization and creating thumbprints.\n///\n/// See [RFC 7518] for the details about the fields for various key types.\n///\n/// [`Self::thumbprint()`] and the [`Display`](fmt::Display) implementation\n/// allow to get the overall presentation of the key. The latter returns JSON serialization\n/// of the key with fields ordered alphabetically. That is, this output for verifying keys\n/// can be used to compute key thumbprints.\n///\n/// # Serialization\n///\n/// For human-readable formats (e.g., JSON, TOML, YAML), byte fields in `JsonWebKey`\n/// and embedded types ([`SecretBytes`], [`RsaPrivateParts`], [`RsaPrimeFactor`]) will be\n/// serialized in base64-url encoding with no padding, as per the JWK spec.\n/// For other formats (e.g., CBOR), byte fields will be serialized as byte sequences.\n///\n/// Because of [the limitations](https://github.com/pyfisch/cbor/issues/3)\n/// of the CBOR support in `serde`, a `JsonWebKey` serialized in CBOR is **not** compliant\n/// with the [CBOR Object Signing and Encryption spec][COSE] (COSE). It can still be a good\n/// way to decrease the serialized key size.\n///\n/// # Conversions\n///\n/// A JWK can be obtained from signing and verifying keys defined in the [`alg`](crate::alg)\n/// module via [`From`] / [`Into`] traits. Conversion from a JWK to a specific key is fallible\n/// and can be performed via [`TryFrom`] with [`JwkError`] as an error\n/// type.\n///\n/// As a part of conversion for asymmetric signing keys, it is checked whether\n/// the signing and verifying parts of the JWK match; [`JwkError::MismatchedKeys`] is returned\n/// otherwise. This check is **not** performed for verifying keys even if the necessary data\n/// is present in the provided JWK.\n///\n/// ⚠ **Warning.** Conversions for private RSA keys are not fully compliant with [RFC 7518].\n/// See the docs for the relevant `impl`s for more details.\n///\n/// [RFC 7518]: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7518#section-6\n/// [JWK]: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7517.html\n/// [COSE]: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc8152\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"kty\")]\n#[non_exhaustive]\npub enum JsonWebKey<'a> {\n    /// Public or private RSA key. Has `kty` field set to `RSA`.\n    #[serde(rename = \"RSA\")]\n    Rsa {\n        /// Key modulus (`n`).\n        #[serde(rename = \"n\", with = \"base64url\")]\n        modulus: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n        /// Public exponent (`e`).\n        #[serde(rename = \"e\", with = \"base64url\")]\n        public_exponent: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n        /// Private RSA parameters. Only present for private keys.\n        #[serde(flatten)]\n        private_parts: Option<RsaPrivateParts<'a>>,\n    },\n    /// Public or private key in an ECDSA crypto system. Has `kty` field set to `EC`.\n    #[serde(rename = \"EC\")]\n    EllipticCurve {\n        /// Curve name (`crv`), such as `secp256k1`.\n        #[serde(rename = \"crv\")]\n        curve: Cow<'a, str>,\n        /// `x` coordinate of the curve point.\n        #[serde(with = \"base64url\")]\n        x: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n        /// `y` coordinate of the curve point.\n        #[serde(with = \"base64url\")]\n        y: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n        /// Secret scalar (`d`); not present for public keys.\n        #[serde(rename = \"d\", default, skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n        secret: Option<SecretBytes<'a>>,\n    },\n    /// Generic symmetric key, e.g. for `HS256` algorithm. Has `kty` field set to `oct`.\n    #[serde(rename = \"oct\")]\n    Symmetric {\n        /// Bytes representing this key.\n        #[serde(rename = \"k\")]\n        secret: SecretBytes<'a>,\n    },\n    /// Generic asymmetric keypair. This key type is used e.g. for Ed25519 keys.\n    #[serde(rename = \"OKP\")]\n    KeyPair {\n        /// Curve name (`crv`), such as `Ed25519`.\n        #[serde(rename = \"crv\")]\n        curve: Cow<'a, str>,\n        /// Public key. For Ed25519, this is the standard 32-byte public key presentation\n        /// (`x` coordinate of a point on the curve + sign).\n        #[serde(with = \"base64url\")]\n        x: Cow<'a, [u8]>,\n        /// Secret key (`d`). For Ed25519, this is the seed.\n        #[serde(rename = \"d\", default, skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n        secret: Option<SecretBytes<'a>>,\n    },\n}\n\nimpl JsonWebKey<'_> {\n    /// Gets the type of this key.\n    pub fn key_type(&self) -> KeyType {\n        match self {\n            Self::Rsa { .. } => KeyType::Rsa,\n            Self::EllipticCurve { .. } => RSA-based JWT algorithms: `RS*` and `PS*`.\n\npub use rsa::{errors::Error as RsaError, RsaPrivateKey, RsaPublicKey};\n\nuse rand_core::{CryptoRng, RngCore};\nuse rsa::{BigUint, Pkcs1v15Sign, Pss, PublicKey, PublicKeyParts};\nuse sha2::{Digest, Sha256, Sha384, Sha512};\n\nuse core::{fmt, str::FromStr};\n\nuse crate::{\n    alg::{SecretBytes, StrongKey, WeakKeyError},\n    alloc::{Box, Cow, String, ToOwned, Vec},\n    jwk::{JsonWebKey, JwkError, KeyType, RsaPrimeFactor, RsaPrivateParts},\n    Algorithm, AlgorithmSignature,\n};\n\n/// RSA signature.\n#[derive(Debug)]\n#[cfg_attr(docsrs, doc(cfg(feature = \"rsa\")))]\npub struct RsaSignature(Vec<u8>);\n\nimpl AlgorithmSignature for RsaSignature {\n    fn try_from_slice(bytes: &[u8]) -> anyhow::Result<Self> {\n        Ok(RsaSignature(bytes.to_vec()))\n    }\n\n    fn as_bytes(&self) -> Cow<'_, [u8]> {\n        Cow::Borrowed(&self.0)\n    }\n}\n\n/// RSA hash algorithm.\n#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, Eq, PartialEq)]\nenum HashAlg {\n    Sha256,\n    Sha384,\n    Sha512,\n}\n\nimpl HashAlg {\n    fn digest(self, message: &[u8]) -> Box<[u8]> {\n        match self {\n            Self::Sha256 => {\n                let digest: [u8; 32] = *(Sha256::digest(message).as_ref());\n                Box::new(digest)\n            }\n            Self::Sha384 => {\n                let mut digest = [0_u8; 48];\n                digest.copy_from_slice(Sha384::digest(message).as_ref());\n                Box::new(digest)\n            }\n            Self::Sha512 => {\n                let mut digest = [0_u8; 64];\n                digest.copy_from_slice(Sha512::digest(message).as_ref());\n                Box::new(digest)\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// RSA padding algorithm.\n#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, Eq, PartialEq)]\nenum Padding {\n    Pkcs1v15,\n    Pss,\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug)]\nenum PaddingScheme {\n    Pkcs1v15(Pkcs1v15Sign),\n    Pss(Pss),\n}\n\n/// Bit length of an RSA key modulus (aka RSA key length).\n#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, Eq, PartialEq)]\n#[non_exhaustive]\n#[cfg_attr(docsrs, doc(cfg(feature = \"rsa\")))]\npub enum ModulusBits {\n    /// 2048 bits. This is the minimum recommended key length as of 2020.\n    TwoKibibytes,\n    /// 3072 bits.\n    ThreeKibibytes,\n    /// 4096 bits.\n    FourKibibytes,\n}\n\nimpl ModulusBits {\n    /// Converts this length to the numeric value.\n    pub fn bits(self) -> usize {\n        match self {\n            Self::TwoKibibytes => 2_048,\n            Self::ThreeKibibytes => 3_072,\n            Self::FourKibibytes => 4_096,\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn is_valid_bits(bits: usize) -> bool {\n        matches!(bits, 2_048 | 3_072 | 4_096)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl TryFrom<usize> for ModulusBits {\n    type Error = ModulusBitsError;\n\n    fn try_from(value: usize) -> Result<Self, Self::Error> {\n        match value {\n            2_048 => Ok(Self::TwoKibibytes),\n            3_072 => Ok(Self::ThreeKibibytes),\n            4_096 => Ok(Self::FourKibibytes),\n            _ => Err(ModulusBitsError(())),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Error type returned when a conversion of an integer into `ModulusBits` fails.\n#[derive(Debug)]\n#[cfg_attr(docsrs, doc(cfg(feature = \"rsa\")))]\npub struct ModulusBitsError(());\n\nimpl fmt::Display for ModulusBitsError {\n    fn fmt(&self, formatter: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        formatter.write_str(\n            \"Unsupported bit length of RSA modulus; only lengths 2048, 3072 and 4096 \\\n            are supported.\",\n        )\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"std\")]\nimpl std::error::Error for ModulusBitsError {}\n\n/// Integrity algorithm using [RSA] digital signatures.\n///\n/// Depending on the variation, the algorithm employs PKCS#1 v1.5 or PSS padding and\n/// one of the hash functions from the SHA-2 family: SHA-256, SHA-384, or SHA-512.\n/// See [RFC 7518] for more details. Depending on the chosen parameters,\n/// the name of the algorithm is one of `RS256`, `RS384`, `RS512`, `PS256`, `PS384`, `PS512`:\n///\n/// - `R` / `P` denote the padding scheme: PKCS#1 v1.5 for `R`, PSS for `P`\n/// - `256` / `384` / `512` denote the hash function\n///\n/// The length of RSA keys is not unequivocally specified by the algorithm; nevertheless,\n/// it **MUST** be at least 2048 bits as per RFC 7518. To minimize risks of misconfiguration,\n/// use [`StrongAlg`](super::StrongAlg) wrapper around `Rsa`:\n///\n/// ```\n/// # use jwt_compact::alg::{StrongAlg, Rsa};\n/// const ALG: StrongAlg<Rsa> = StrongAlg(Rsa::rs256());\n/// // `ALG` will not support RSA keys with unsecure lengths by design!\n/// ```\n///\n/// [RSA]: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RSA_(cryptosystem)\n/// [RFC 7518]: https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7518.html\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\n#[cfg_attr(docsrs, doc(cfg(feature = \"rsa\")))]\npub struct Rsa {\n    hash_alg: HashAlg,\n    padding_alg: Padding,\n}\n\nimpl Algorithm for Rsa {\n    type SigningKey = RsaPrivateKey;\n    type VerifyingKey = RsaPublicKey;\n    type Signature = RsaSignature;\n\n    fn name(&self) -> Cow<'static, str> {\n        Cow::Borrowed(self.alg_name())\n    }\n\n    fn sign(&self, signing_key: &Self::SigningKey, message: &[u8]) -> Self::Signature {\n        let digest = self.hash_alg.digest(message);\n        let signing_result = match self.padding_scheme() {\n            PaddingScheme::Pkcs1v15(padding) => {\n                signing_key.sign_with_rng(&mut rand_core::OsRng, padding, &digest)\n            }\n            PaddingScheme::Pss(padding) => {\n                signing_key.sign_with_rng(&mut rand_core::OsRng, padding, &digest)\n            }\n        };\n        RsaSignature(signing_result.expect(\"Unexpected RSA signature failure\"))\n    }\n\n    fn verify_signature(\n        &self,\n        signature: &Self::Signature,\n        verifying_key: &Self::VerifyingKey,\n        message: &[u8],\n    ) -> bool {\n        let digest = self.hash_alg.digest(message);\n        let verify_result = match self.padding_scheme() {\n            PaddingScheme::Pkcs1v15(padding) => {\n                verifying_key.verify(padding, &digest, &signature.0)\n            }\n            PaddingScheme::Pss(padding) => verifying_key.verify(padding, &digest, &signature.0),\n        };\n        verify_result.is_ok()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Rsa {\n    const fn new(hash_alg: HashAlg, padding_alg: Padding) -> Self {\n        Rsa {\n            hash_alg,\n            padding_alg,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// RSA with SHA-256 and PKCS#1 v1.5 padding.\n    pub const fn rs256() -> Rsa {\n        Rsa::new(HashAlg::Sha256, Padding::Pkcs1v15)\n    }\n\n    /// RSA with SHA-384 and PKCS#1 v1.5 padding.\n    pub const fn rs384() -> Rsa {\n        Rsa::new(HashAlg::Sha384, Padding::Pkcs1v15)\n    }\n\n    /// RSA with SHA-512 and PKCS#1 v1.5 padding.\n    pub const fn rs512() -> Rsa {\n        Rsa::new(HashAlg::Sha512, Padding::Pkcs1v15)\n    }\n\n    /// RSA with SHA-256 and PSS padding.\n    pub const fn ps256() -> Rsa {\n        Rsa::new(HashAlg::Sha256, Padding::Pss)\n    }\n\n    /// RSA with SHA-384 and PSS padding.\n    pub const fn ps384() -> Rsa {\n        Rsa::new(HashAlg::Sha384, Padding::Pss)\n    }\n\n    /// RSA with SHA-512 and PSS padding.\n    pub const fn ps512() -> Rsa {\n        Rsa::new(HashAlg::Sha512, Padding::Pss)\n    }\n\n    /// RSA based on the specified algorithm name.\n    ///\n    /// # Panics\n    ///\n    /// - Panics if the name is not one of the six RSA-based JWS algorithms. Prefer using\n    ///   the [`FromStr`] trait if the conversion is potentially fallible.\n    pub fn with_name(name: &str) -> Self {\n        name.parse().unwrap()\n    }\n\n    fn padding_scheme(self) -> PaddingScheme {\n        match self.padding_alg {\n            Padding::Pkcs1v15 => PaddingScheme::Pkcs1v15(match self.hash_alg {\n                HashAlg::Sha256 => Pkcs1v15Sign::new::<Sha256>(),\n                HashAlg::Sha384 => Pkcs1v15Sign::new::<Sha384>(),\n                HashAlg::Sha512 => Pkcs1v15Sign::new::<Sha512>(),\n            }),\n            Padding::Pss => {\n                // The salt length needs to be set to the size of hash function output;\n                // see https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7518.html#section-3.5.\n                PaddingScheme::Pss(match self.hash_alg {\n                    HashAlg::Sha256 => Pss::new_with_salt::<Sha256>(Sha256::output_size()),\n                    HashAlg::Sha384 => Pss::new_with_salt::<Sha384>(Sha384::output_size()),\n                    HashAlg::Sha512 => Pss::new_with_salt::<Sha512>(Sha512::output_size()),\n                })\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn alg_name(self) -> &'static str {\n        match (self.padding_alg, self.hash_alg) {\n            (Padding::Pkcs1v15, HashAlg::Sha256) => \"RS256\",\n            (Padding::Pkcs1v15, HashAlg::Sha384) => \"RS384\",\n            (Padding::Pkcs1v15, HashAlg::Sha512) => \"RS512\",\n            (Padding::Pss, HashAlg::Sha256) => \"PS256\",\n            (Padding::Pss, HashAlg::Sha384) => \"PS384\",\n            (Padding::Pss, HashAlg::Sha512) => \"PS512\",\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Generates a new key pair with the specified modulus bit length (aka key length).\n    pub fn generate<R: CryptoRng + RngCore>(\n        rng: &mut R,\n        modulus_bits: ModulusBits,\n    ) -> rsa::errors::Result<(StrongKey<RsaPrivateKey>, StrongKey<RsaPublicKey>)> {\n        let signing_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(rng, modulus_bits.bits())?;\n        let verifying_key = signing_key.to_public_key();\n        Ok((StrongKey(signing_key), StrongKey(verifying_key)))\n    }\n}\n\nimpl FromStr for Rsa {\n    type Err = RsaParseError;\n\n    fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {\n        Ok(match s {\n            \"RS256\" => Self::rs256(),\n            \"RS384\" => Self::rs384(),\n            \"RS512\" => Self::rs512(),\n            \"PS256\" => Self::ps256(),\n            \"PS384\" => Self::ps384(),\n            \"PS512\" => Self::ps512(),\n            _ => return Err(RsaParseError(s.to_owned())),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n/// Errors that can occur when parsing an [`Rsa`] algorithm from a string.\n#[derive(Debug)]\n#[cfg_attr(docsrs, doc(cfg(feature = \"rsa\")))]\npub struct RsaParseError(String);\n\nimpl fmt::Display for RsaParseError {\n    fn fmt(&self, formatter: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        write!(formatter, \"Invalid RSA algorithm name: {}\", self.0)\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"std\")]\nimpl std::error::Error for RsaParseError {}\n\nimpl StrongKey<RsaPrivateKey> {\n    /// Converts this private key to a public key.\n    pub fn to_public_key(&self) -> StrongKey<RsaPublicKey> {\n        StrongKey(self.0.to_public_key())\n    }\n}\n\nimpl TryFrom<RsaPrivateKey> for StrongKey<RsaPrivateKey> {\n    type Error = WeakKeyError<RsaPrivateKey>;\n\n    fn try_from(key: RsaPrivateKey) -> Result<Self, Self::Error> {\n        if ModulusBits::is_valid_bits(key.n().bits()) {\n            Ok(StrongKey(key))\n        } else {\n            Err(WeakKeyError(key))\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl TryFrom<RsaPublicKey> for StrongKey<RsaPublicKey> {\n    type Error = WeakKeyError<RsaPublicKey>;\n\n    fn try_from(key: RsaPublicKey) -> Result<Self, Self::Error> {\n        if ModulusBits::is_valid_bits(key.n().bits()) {\n            Ok(StrongKey(key))\n        } else {\n            Err(WeakKeyError(key))\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<'a> From<&'a RsaPublicKey> for JsonWebKey<'a> {\n    fn from(key: &'a RsaPublicKey) -> JsonWebKey<'a> {\n        JsonWebKey::Rsa {\n            modulus: Cow::Owned(key.n().to_bytes_be()),\n            public_exponent: Cow::Owned(key.e().to_bytes_be()),\n            private_parts: None,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl TryFrom<&JsonWebKey<'_>> for RsaPublicKey {\n    type Error = JwkError;\n\n    fn try_from(jwk: &JsonWebKey<'_>) -> Result<Self, Self::Error> {\n        let JsonWebKey::Rsa {\n            modulus,\n            public_exponent,\n            ..\n        } = jwk else {\n            return Err(JwkError::key_type(jwk, KeyType::Rsa));\n        };\n\n        let e = BigUint::from_bytes_be(public_exponent);\n        let n = BigUint::from_bytes_be(modulus);\n        Self::new(n, e).map_err(|err| JwkError::custom(anyhow::anyhow!(err)))\n    }\n}\n\n/// ⚠ **Warning.** Contrary to [RFC 7518], this implementation does not set `dp`, `dq`, and `qi`\n/// fields in the JWK root object, as well as `d` and `t` fields for additional factors\n/// (i.e., in the `oth` array).\n///\n/// [RFC 7518]: https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7518#section-6.3.2\nimpl<'a> From<&'a RsaPrivateKey> for JsonWebKey<'a> {\n    fn from(key: &'a RsaPrivateKey) -> JsonWebKey<'a> {\n        const MSG: &str = \"RsaPrivateKey must have at least 2 prime factors\";\n\n        let p = key.primes().get(0).expect(MSG);\n        let q = key.primes().get(1).expect(MSG);\n\n        let private_parts = RsaPrivateParts {\n            private_exponent: SecretBytes::owned(key.d().to_bytes_be()),\n            prime_factor_p: SecretBytes::owned(p.to_bytes_be()),\n            prime_factor_q: SecretBytes::owned(q.to_bytes_be()),\n            p_crt_exponent: None,\n            q_crt_exponent: None,\n            q_crt_coefficient: None,\n            other_prime_factors: key.primes()[2..]\n                .iter()\n                .map(|factor| RsaPrimeFactor {\n                    factor: SecretBytes::owned(factor.to_bytes_be()),\n                    crt_exponent: None,\n                    crt_coefficient: None,\n                })\n                .collect(),\n        };\n\n        JsonWebKey::Rsa {\n            modulus: Cow::Owned(key.n().to_bytes_be()),\n            public_exponent: Cow::Owned(key.e().to_bytes_be()),\n            private_parts: Some(private_parts),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// ⚠ **Warning.** Contrary to [RFC 7518] (at least, in spirit), this conversion ignores\n/// `dp`, `dq`, and `qi` fields from JWK, as well as `d` and `t` fields for additional factors.\n///\n/// [RFC 7518]: https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7518.html\nimpl   [Clippy Documentation](https://doc.rust-lang.org/nightly/clippy)\n\n[![](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/RustCrypto/meta/master/logo_small.png)](../rsa/index.html)\n\n[![logo](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/RustCrypto/meta/master/logo_small.png)](../rsa/index.html)\n\n## [rsa](../rsa/index.html)0.9.8\n\n-   [All Items](all.html)\n\n### [Sections](#)\n\n-   [RustCrypto: RSA](#rustcrypto-rsa \"RustCrypto: RSA\")\n    -   [PKCS#1 RSA Key Encoding](#pkcs1-rsa-key-encoding \"PKCS#1 RSA Key Encoding\")\n    -   [PKCS#8 RSA Key Encoding](#pkcs8-rsa-key-encoding \"PKCS#8 RSA Key Encoding\")\n\n### [Crate Items](#reexports)\n\n-   [Re-exports](#reexports \"Re-exports\")\n-   [Modules](#modules \"Modules\")\n-   [Structs](#structs \"Structs\")\n\n# Crate rsaCopy item path\n\n[Source](../src/rsa/lib.rs.html#1-255)\n\nExpand description\n\n## [§](#rustcrypto-rsa)[RustCrypto](https://github.com/RustCrypto/): RSA\n\n[![crates.io](https://buildstats.info/crate/rsa)](https://crates.io/crates/rsa) [![Documentation](https://docs.rs/rsa/badge.svg)](https://docs.rs/rsa) [![Build Status](https://github.com/rustcrypto/RSA/workflows/CI/badge.svg)](https://github.com/RustCrypto/RSA/actions?query=workflow%3ACI+branch%3Amaster) [![dependency status](https://deps.rs/repo/github/RustCrypto/RSA/status.svg)](https://deps.rs/repo/github/RustCrypto/RSA) ![MSRV](https://img.shields.io/badge/rustc-1.65+-blue.svg) [![Project Chat](https://img.shields.io/badge/zulip-join_chat-blue.svg)](https://rustcrypto.zulipchat.com/#narrow/stream/260047-RSA)\n\nA portable RSA implementation in pure Rust.\n\n### [§](#example)Example\n\n```\nuse rsa::{Pkcs1v15Encrypt, RsaPrivateKey, RsaPublicKey};\n\nlet mut rng = rand::thread_rng(); // rand@0.8\nlet bits = 2048;\nlet priv_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\nlet pub_key = RsaPublicKey::from(&priv_key);\n\n// Encrypt\nlet data = b\"hello world\";\nlet enc_data = pub_key.encrypt(&mut rng, Pkcs1v15Encrypt, &data[..]).expect(\"failed to encrypt\");\nassert_ne!(&data[..], &enc_data[..]);\n\n// Decrypt\nlet dec_data = priv_key.decrypt(Pkcs1v15Encrypt, &enc_data).expect(\"failed to decrypt\");\nassert_eq!(&data[..], &dec_data[..]);\n```\n\n> **Note:** If you encounter unusually slow key generation time while using `RsaPrivateKey::new` you can try to compile in release mode or add the following to your `Cargo.toml`. Key generation is much faster when building with higher optimization levels, but this will increase the compile time a bit.\n> \n> ```\n> [profile.debug]\n> opt-level = 3\n> ```\n> \n> If you don’t want to turn on optimizations for all dependencies, you can only optimize the `num-bigint-dig` dependency. This should give most of the speedups.\n> \n> ```\n> [profile.dev.package.num-bigint-dig]\n> opt-level = 3\n> ```\n\n### [§](#status)Status\n\nCurrently at Phase 1 (v) 🚧\n\nThere will be three phases before `1.0` 🚢 can be released.\n\n1.  🚧 Make it work\n    -    Prime generation ✅\n    -    Key generation ✅\n    -    PKCS1v1.5: Encryption & Decryption ✅\n    -    PKCS1v1.5: Sign & Verify ✅\n    -    PKCS1v1.5 (session key): Encryption & Decryption\n    -    OAEP: Encryption & Decryption\n    -    PSS: Sign & Verify\n    -    Key import & export\n2.  🚀 Make it fast\n    -    Benchmarks ✅\n    -    compare to other implementations 🚧\n    -    optimize 🚧\n3.  🔐 Make it secure\n    -    Fuzz testing\n    -    Security Audits\n\n### [§](#security-warning)⚠️Security Warning\n\nThis crate has received one [security audit by Include Security](https://www.opentech.fund/results/security-safety-audits/deltachat/), with only one minor finding which has since been addressed.\n\nSee the [open security issues](https://github.com/RustCrypto/RSA/issues?q=is%3Aissue+is%3Aopen+label%3Asecurity) on our issue tracker for other known problems.\n\nNotably the implementation of [modular exponentiation is not constant time](https://github.com/RustCrypto/RSA/issues/19), but timing variability is masked using [random blinding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blinding_\\(cryptography\\)), a commonly used technique. This crate is vulnerable to the [Marvin Attack](https://people.redhat.com/~hkario/marvin/) which could enable private key recovery by a network attacker (see [RUSTSEC-2023-0071](https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2023-0071.html)).\n\nYou can follow our work on mitigating this issue in [#390](https://github.com/RustCrypto/RSA/issues/390).\n\n### [§](#minimum-supported-rust-version-msrv)Minimum Supported Rust Version (MSRV)\n\nAll crates in this repository support Rust 1.65 or higher.\n\nIn the future MSRV can be changed, but it will be done with a minor version bump.\n\n### [§](#license)License\n\nLicensed under either of\n\n-   [Apache License, Version 2.0](http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0)\n-   [MIT license](http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)\n\nat your option.\n\n#### [§](#contribution)Contribution\n\nUnless you explicitly state otherwise, any contribution intentionally submitted for inclusion in the work by you, as defined in the Apache-2.0 license, shall be dual licensed as above, without any additional terms or conditions.\n\n## [§](#supported-algorithms)Supported algorithms\n\nThis crate supports several schemes described in [RFC8017](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8017#section-8.1):\n\n-   [OAEP encryption scheme](#oaep-encryption)\n-   [PKCS#1 v1.5 encryption scheme](#pkcs1-v15-encryption)\n-   [PKCS#1 v1.5 signature scheme](#pkcs1-v15-signatures)\n-   [PSS signature scheme](#pss-signatures)\n\nThese schemes are described below.\n\n## [§](#usage)Usage\n\n### [§](#oaep-encryption)OAEP encryption\n\nNote: requires `sha2` feature of `rsa` crate is enabled.\n\n```\nuse rsa::{RsaPrivateKey, RsaPublicKey, Oaep, sha2::Sha256};\n\nlet mut rng = rand::thread_rng(); // rand@0.8\n\nlet bits = 2048;\nlet private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\nlet public_key = RsaPublicKey::from(&private_key);\n\n// Encrypt\nlet data = b\"hello world\";\nlet padding = Oaep::new::<Sha256>();\nlet enc_data = public_key.encrypt(&mut rng, padding, &data[..]).expect(\"failed to encrypt\");\nassert_ne!(&data[..], &enc_data[..]);\n\n// Decrypt\nlet padding = Oaep::new::<Sha256>();\nlet dec_data = private_key.decrypt(padding, &enc_data).expect(\"failed to decrypt\");\nassert_eq!(&data[..], &dec_data[..]);\n```\n\n### [§](#pkcs1-v15-encryption)PKCS#1 v1.5 encryption\n\n```\nuse rsa::{RsaPrivateKey, RsaPublicKey, Pkcs1v15Encrypt};\n\nlet mut rng = rand::thread_rng(); // rand@0.8\n\nlet bits = 2048;\nlet private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\nlet public_key = RsaPublicKey::from(&private_key);\n\n// Encrypt\nlet data = b\"hello world\";\nlet enc_data = public_key.encrypt(&mut rng, Pkcs1v15Encrypt, &data[..]).expect(\"failed to encrypt\");\nassert_ne!(&data[..], &enc_data[..]);\n\n// Decrypt\nlet dec_data = private_key.decrypt(Pkcs1v15Encrypt, &enc_data).expect(\"failed to decrypt\");\nassert_eq!(&data[..], &dec_data[..]);\n```\n\n### [§](#pkcs1-v15-signatures)PKCS#1 v1.5 signatures\n\nNote: requires `sha2` feature of `rsa` crate is enabled.\n\n```\nuse rsa::RsaPrivateKey;\nuse rsa::pkcs1v15::{SigningKey, VerifyingKey};\nuse rsa::signature::{Keypair, RandomizedSigner, SignatureEncoding, Verifier};\nuse rsa::sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n\nlet mut rng = rand::thread_rng(); // rand@0.8\n\nlet bits = 2048;\nlet private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\nlet signing_key = SigningKey::<Sha256>::new(private_key);\nlet verifying_key = signing_key.verifying_key();\n\n// Sign\nlet data = b\"hello world\";\nlet signature = signing_key.sign_with_rng(&mut rng, data);\nassert_ne!(signature.to_bytes().as_ref(), data.as_slice());\n\n// Verify\nverifying_key.verify(data, &signature).expect(\"failed to verify\");\n```\n\n### [§](#pss-signatures)PSS signatures\n\nNote: requires `sha2` feature of `rsa` crate is enabled.\n\n```\nuse rsa::RsaPrivateKey;\nuse rsa::pss::{BlindedSigningKey, VerifyingKey};\nuse rsa::signature::{Keypair,RandomizedSigner, SignatureEncoding, Verifier};\nuse rsa::sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n\nlet mut rng = rand::thread_rng(); // rand@0.8\n\nlet bits = 2048;\nlet private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\nlet signing_key = BlindedSigningKey::<Sha256>::new(private_key);\nlet verifying_key = signing_key.verifying_key();\n\n// Sign\nlet data = b\"hello world\";\nlet signature = signing_key.sign_with_rng(&mut rng, data);\nassert_ne!(signature.to_bytes().as_ref(), data);\n\n// Verify\nverifying_key.verify(data, &signature).expect(\"failed to verify\");\n```\n\n### [§](#pkcs1-rsa-key-encoding)PKCS#1 RSA Key Encoding\n\nPKCS#1 supports a legacy format for encoding RSA keys as binary (DER) or text (PEM) data.\n\nYou can recognize PEM encoded PKCS#1 keys because they have “RSA \\* KEY” in the type label, e.g.:\n\n```\n-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----\n```\n\nMost modern applications use the newer PKCS#8 format instead (see below).\n\nThe following traits can be used to decode/encode [`RsaPrivateKey`](struct.RsaPrivateKey.html \"struct rsa::RsaPrivateKey\") and [`RsaPublicKey`](struct.RsaPublicKey.html \"struct rsa::RsaPublicKey\") as PKCS#1. Note that [`pkcs1`](https://docs.rs/pkcs1/0.7.5/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/pkcs1/index.html \"mod pkcs1\") is re-exported from the toplevel of the `rsa` crate:\n\n-   [`pkcs1::DecodeRsaPrivateKey`](https://docs.rs/pkcs1/0.7.5/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/pkcs1/traits/trait.DecodeRsaPrivateKey.html \"trait pkcs1::traits::DecodeRsaPrivateKey\"): decode RSA private keys from PKCS#1\n-   [`pkcs1::EncodeRsaPrivateKey`](https://docs.rs/pkcs1/0.7.5/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/pkcs1/traits/trait.EncodeRsaPrivateKey.html \"trait pkcs1::traits::EncodeRsaPrivateKey\"): encode RSA private keys to PKCS#1\n-   [`pkcs1::DecodeRsaPublicKey`](https://docs.rs/pkcs1/0.7.5/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/pkcs1/traits/trait.DecodeRsaPublicKey.html \"trait pkcs1::traits::DecodeRsaPublicKey\"): decode RSA public keys from PKCS#1\n-   [`pkcs1::EncodeRsaPublicKey`](https://docs.rs/pkcs1/0.7.5/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/pkcs1/traits/trait.EncodeRsaPublicKey.html \"trait pkcs1::traits::EncodeRsaPublicKey\"): encode RSA public keys to PKCS#1\n\n#### [§](#example-1)Example\n\n```\nuse rsa::{RsaPublicKey, pkcs1::DecodeRsaPublicKey};\n\nlet pem = \"-----BEGIN RSA PUBLIC KEY-----\nMIIBCgKCAQEAtsQsUV8QpqrygsY+2+JCQ6Fw8/omM71IM2N/R8pPbzbgOl0p78MZ\nGsgPOQ2HSznjD0FPzsH8oO2B5Uftws04LHb2HJAYlz25+lN5cqfHAfa3fgmC38Ff\nwBkn7l582UtPWZ/wcBOnyCgb3yLcvJrXyrt8QxHJgvWO23ITrUVYszImbXQ67YGS\n0YhMrbixRzmo2tpm3JcIBtnHrEUMsT0NfFdfsZhTT8YbxBvA8FdODgEwx7u/vf3J\n9qbi4+Kv8cvqyJuleIRSjVXPsIMnoejIn04APPKIjpMyQdnWlby7rNyQtE4+CV+j\ncFjqJbE/Xilcvqxt6DirjFCvYeKYl1uHLwIDAQAB\n-----END RSA PUBLIC KEY-----\";\n\nlet public_key = RsaPublicKey::from_pkcs1_pem(pem)?;\n```\n\n### [§](#pkcs8-rsa-key-encoding)PKCS#8 RSA Key Encoding\n\nPKCS#8 is a private key format with support for multiple algorithms. Like PKCS#1, it can be encoded as binary (DER) or text (PEM).\n\nYou can recognize PEM encoded PKCS#8 keys because they *don’t* have an algorithm name in the type label, e.g.:\n\n```\n-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----\n```\n\nThe following traits can be used to decode/encode [`RsaPrivateKey`](struct.RsaPrivateKey.html \"struct rsa::RsaPrivateKey\") and [`RsaPublicKey`](struct.RsaPublicKey.html \"struct rsa::RsaPublicKey\") as PKCS#8. Note that [`pkcs8`](https://docs.rs/pkcs8/0.10.2/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/pkcs8/index.html \"mod pkcs8\") is re-exported from the toplevel of the `rsa` crate:\n\n-   [`pkcs8::DecodePrivateKey`](https://docs.rs/pkcs8/0.10.2/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/pkcs8/traits/trait.DecodePrivateKey.html \"trait pkcs8::traits::DecodePrivateKey\"): decode private keys from PKCS#8\n-   [`pkcs8::EncodePrivateKey`](https://docs.rs/pkcs8/0.10.2/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/pkcs8/traits/trait.EncodePrivateKey.html \"trait pkcs8::traits::EncodePrivateKey\"): encode private keys to PKCS#8\n-   [`pkcs8::DecodePublicKey`](https://docs.rs/pkcs8/latest/pkcs8/trait.DecodePublicKey.html): decode public keys from PKCS#8\n-   [`pkcs8::EncodePublicKey`](https://docs.rs/pkcs8/latest/pkcs8/trait.EncodePublicKey.html): encode public keys to PKCS#8\n\n#### [§](#example-2)Example\n\n```\nuse rsa::{RsaPublicKey, pkcs8::DecodePublicKey};\n\nlet pem = \"-----BEGIN PUBLIC KEY-----\nMIIBIjANBgkqhkiG9w0BAQEFAAOCAQ8AMIIBCgKCAQEAtsQsUV8QpqrygsY+2+JC\nQ6Fw8/omM71IM2N/R8pPbzbgOl0p78MZGsgPOQ2HSznjD0FPzsH8oO2B5Uftws04\nLHb2HJAYlz25+lN5cqfHAfa3fgmC38FfwBkn7l582UtPWZ/wcBOnyCgb3yLcvJrX\nyrt8QxHJgvWO23ITrUVYszImbXQ67YGS0YhMrbixRzmo2tpm3JcIBtnHrEUMsT0N\nfFdfsZhTT8YbxBvA8FdODgEwx7u/vf3J9qbi4+Kv8cvqyJuleIRSjVXPsIMnoejI\nn04APPKIjpMyQdnWlby7rNyQtE4+CV+jcFjqJbE/Xilcvqxt6DirjFCvYeKYl1uH\nLwIDAQAB\n-----END PUBLIC KEY-----\";\n\nlet public_key = RsaPublicKey::from_public_key_pem(pem)?;\n```\n\n## Re-exports[§](#reexports)\n\n`pub use crate::errors::[Error](errors/enum.Error.html \"enum {}\n53impl PartialEq for RsaPrivateKey {\n54    #[inline]\n55    fn eq(&self, other: &RsaPrivateKey) -> bool {\n56        self.pubkey_components == other.pubkey_components\n57            && self.d == {\n88    /// D mod (P-1)\n89    pub(crate) dp: BigUint,\n90    /// D mod (Q-1)\n91    pub(crate) dq: BigUint,\n92    /// Q^-1 mod P\n93    pub(crate) qinv: BigInt,\n94\n95    /// CRTValues is used for the 3rd and subsequent primes.    pub(crate) crt_values: Vec<CrtValue>,\n100}\n101\n102impl Zeroize for PrecomputedValues {\n103    fn zeroize(&mut self) {\n104        self.dp.zeroize();\n105        self.dq.zeroize();\n106 private_key.n().clone();\n129        let e = private_key.e().clone();\n130        RsaPublicKey { n, e }\n131    }\n132}\n133\n134impl PublicKeyParts for RsaPublicKey {\n135    fn n(&self) -> &BigUint {\n136        &self.n\n137    }\n138\n139    fn e(&self) -> &BigUint {\n140        &self.e\n141    }\n142}\n143\n144impl RsaPublicKey {\n145    /// Encrypt the given message.\n146    pub fn encrypt<R: CryptoRngCore, P: PaddingScheme>(\n147        &self,\n148        rng: &mut R,\n149        padding: P,\n150        msg: &[u8],\n151    ) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {\n152        padding.encrypt(rng, self, msg)\n153    }\n154\n155    /// Verify a signed message.\n156    ///\n157    /// `hashed` must be the result of hashing the input using the hashing function\n158    /// passed in through `hash`.\n159    ///\n160    /// If the message is valid `Ok(())` is returned, otherwise an `Err` indicating failure.\n161    pub fn verify<S: SignatureScheme>(&self, scheme: S, hashed: &[u8], sig: &[u8]) -> Result<()> {\n162        scheme.verify(self, hashed, sig)\n163    }\n164}\n165\n166impl RsaPublicKey {\n167    /// Minimum value of the public exponent `e`.\n168    pub const MIN_PUB_EXPONENT: u64 = 2;\n169\n170    /// Maximum value of the public exponent `e`.\n171    pub const MAX_PUB_EXPONENT: u64 = (1 << 33) - 1;\n172\n173    /// Maximum size of the modulus `n` in bits.\n174    pub const MAX_SIZE: usize = 4096;\n175\n176    /// Create a new public key from its components.\n177    ///\n178    /// This function accepts public keys with a modulus size up to 4096-bits,\n179    /// i.e. from its components.\n185    pub fn new_with_max_size(n: BigUint, e: BigUint, max_size: usize) -> Result<Self> {\n186        let k = Self { n, e };\n187        check_public_with_max_size(&k, max_size)?;\n188        Ok(k)\n189    }\n190\n191    /// Create a new public key, bypassing checks around the modulus and public\n192    /// exponent size.\n193    ///\n194    /// This method is not recommended, and only intended for unusual use cases.\n195    /// Most applications should use [`RsaPublicKey::new`] or\n196    /// [`RsaPublicKey::new_with_max_size`] instead.\n197    pub fn new_unchecked(n: BigUint, e: BigUint) -> Self {\n198        Self { n, e }\n199    }\n200}\n201\n202impl PublicKeyParts for RsaPrivateKey {\n203    fn n(&self) -> &BigUint {\n204 keys.\n214    const EXP: u64 = 65537;\n215\n216    /// Generate a new Rsa key pair of the given bit size using the passed in `rng`.\n217    pub fn new<R: CryptoRngCore + ?Sized>(rng: &mut R, bit_size: usize) -> Result<RsaPrivateKey> {\n218        let exp = BigUint::from_u64(Self::EXP).expect(\"invalid static exponent\");\n219        Self::new_with_exp(rng, bit_size, &exp)\n220    }\n221\n222    /// Generate a new RSA key pair of the given bit size and the public exponent\n223    /// using the passed in `rng`.\n224    ///\n225    /// Unless you have specific needs, you should use `RsaPrivateKey::new` instead.\n226    pub fn new_with_exp<R: CryptoRngCore + ?Sized>(\n227        rng: &mut R,\n228        bit_size: usize,\n229        exp: &BigUint,\n230    ) -> Result<RsaPrivateKey> {\n231        let components = generate_multi_prime_key_with_exp(rng, 2, bit_size, exp)?;\n232        RsaPrivateKey::from_components(components.n, components.e, components.d, components.primes)\n233    }\n234\n235    /// Constructs an RSA key pair from individual components:\n236    ///\n237    /// - `n`: RSA modulus\n238    /// - `e`: public exponent (i.e. encrypting exponent)\n239    /// - `d`: private exponent (i.e. decrypting exponent)\n240    /// - `primes`: prime factors of `n`: typically two primes `p` and `q`. More than two primes can\n241    ///   be provided for multiprime RSA, however this is generally not recommended. If no `primes`\n242    ///   are provided, a prime factor recovery algorithm will be employed to attempt to recover the\n243    ///   factors (as described in [NIST SP 800-56B Revision 2] Appendix C.2). This algorithm only\n244    ///   works if there are just two prime factors `p` and `q` (as opposed to multiprime), and `e`\n245    ///   is between 2^16 and 2^256.\n246    ///\n247    ///  [NIST SP 800-56B Revision 2]: https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.800-56Br2.pdf\n248    pub fn from_components(\n249        n: BigUint,\n250        e: BigUint,\n251        d: BigUint,\n252        mut primes: Vec<BigUint>,\n253    ) -> Result<RsaPrivateKey> {\n254        if primes.len() < 2 {\n255            if !primes.is_empty() {\n256                return Err(Error::NprimesTooSmall);\n257            }\n258            // Recover `p` and `q` from `d`.\n259            // See method in Appendix C.2: https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.800-56Br2.pdf\n260            let (p, q) = recover_primes(&n, &e, &d)?;\n261            primes.push(p);\n262            primes.push(q);\n263        }\n264\n265        let mut k = RsaPrivateKey {\n266            pubkey_components: RsaPublicKey { n, e },\n267            d,\n268            primes,\n269            precomputed: None,\n270        };\n271\n272        // Alaways validate the key, to ensure precompute can't fail\n273        k.validate()?;\n274\n275        // precompute when possible, ignore error otherwise.\n276        let _ = k.precompute();\n277\n278        Ok(k)\n279    }\n280\n281    /// Constructs an RSA key pair from its two primes p and q.\n282    ///\n283    /// This will rebuild the private exponent and the modulus.\n284    ///\n285    /// Private exponent will be rebuilt using the method defined in\n286    /// [NIST 800-56B Section 6.2.1](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.800-56Br2.pdf#page=47).\n287    pub fn from_p_q(p: BigUint, q: BigUint, public_exponent: BigUint) -> Result<RsaPrivateKey> {\n288        if p == q {\n289            return Err(Error::InvalidPrime);\n290        }\n291\n292        let n = compute_modulus(&[p.clone(), q.clone()]);\n293        let d = compute_private_exponent_carmicheal(&p, &q, &public_exponent)?;\n294\n295        Self::from_components(n, public_exponent, d, vec![p, q])\n296    }\n297\n298    /// Constructs an RSA key pair from its primes.\n299    ///\n300    /// This will rebuild the private exponent and the modulus.\n301    pub fn from_primes(primes: Vec<BigUint>, public_exponent: BigUint) -> Result<RsaPrivateKey> {\n302        if primes.len() < 2 {\n303            return Err(Error::NprimesTooSmall);\n304        }\n305\n306        // Makes sure that primes is pairwise unequal.\n307        for (i, prime1) in primes.iter().enumerate() {\n308            for prime2 in primes.iter().take(i) {\n309                if prime1 == prime2 {\n310                    return Err(Error::InvalidPrime);\n311                }\n312            }\n313        }\n314\n315        let n = compute_modulus(&primes);\n316        let d = compute_private_exponent_euler_totient(&primes, &public_exponent)?;\n317\n318        Self::from_components(n, public_exponent, d, primes)\n319    }\n320\n321    /// Get the public key from the private key, cloning `n` and `e`.\n322    ///\n323    /// Generally this is not needed since `RsaPrivateKey` implements the `PublicKey` trait,\n324    /// but it can occasionally be useful to discard the private information entirely.\n325    pub fn to_public_key(&self) -> RsaPublicKey {\n326        self.pubkey_components.clone()\n327    }\n328\n329    /// Performs some calculations to speed up private key operations.\n330    pub fn precompute(&mut self) -> Result<()> {\n331        if self.precomputed.is_some() {\n332            return Ok(());\n333        }\n334\n335        let dp = &self.d % (&self.primes[0] - BigUint::one());\n336        let dq = &self.d % (&self.primes[1] - BigUint::one());\n337        let qinv = self.primes[1]\n338            .clone()\n339            .mod_inverse(&self.primes[0])\n340            .ok_or(Error::InvalidPrime)?;\n341\n342        let mut r: BigUint = &self.primes[0] * &self.primes[1];\n343        let crt_values: Vec<CrtValue> = {\n344            let mut values = Vec::with_capacity(self.primes.len() - 2);\n345            for prime in &self.primes[2..] {\n346                let res = CrtValue {\n347                    exp: BigInt::from_biguint(Plus, &self.d % (prime - BigUint::one())),\n348                    r: BigInt::from_biguint(Plus, r.clone()),\n349                    coeff: BigInt::from_biguint(\n350                        Plus,\n351                        r.clone()\n352                            .mod_inverse(prime)\n353                            .ok_or(Error::InvalidCoefficient)?\n354                            .to_biguint()\n355                            .unwrap(),\n356                    ),\n357                };\n358                r *= prime;\n359\n360                values.push(res);\n361            }\n362            values\n363        };\n364\n365        self.precomputed = Some(PrecomputedValues {\n366            dp,\n367            dq,\n368            qinv,\n369            crt_values,\n370        });\n371\n372        Ok(())\n373    }\n374\n375    /// Clears precomputed values by setting to None\n376    pub fn clear_precomputed(&mut self) {\n377        self.precomputed = None;\n378    }\n379\n380    /// Compute CRT coefficient: `(1/q) mod p`.\n381    pub fn crt_coefficient(&self) -> Option<BigUint> {\n382        (&self.primes[1]).mod_inverse(&self.primes[0])?.to_biguint()\n383    }\n384\n385    /// Performs basic sanity checks on the key.\n386    /// Returns `Ok(())` if everything is good, otherwise an appropriate error.\n387    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<()> {\n388        check_public(self)?;\n389\n390        // Check that Πprimes == n.\n391        let mut m = BigUint::one();\n392        for prime in &self.primes {\n393            // Any primes ≤ 1 will cause divide-by-zero panics later.\n394            if *prime < BigUint::one() {\n395                return Err(Error::InvalidPrime);\n396            }\n397            m *= prime;\n398        }\n399        if m != self.pubkey_components.n {\n400            return Err(Error::InvalidModulus);\n401        }\n402\n403        // Check that de ≡ 1 mod p-1, for each prime.\n404        // This implies that e is coprime to each p-1 as e has a multiplicative\n405        // inverse. Therefore e is coprime to lcm(p-1,q-1,r-1,...) =\n406        // exponent(ℤ/nℤ). It also implies that a^de ≡ a mod p as a^(p-1) ≡ 1\n407        // mod p. Thus a^de ≡ a mod n for all a coprime to n, as required.\n408        let mut de = self.e().clone();\n409        de *= self.d.clone();\n410        for prime in &self.primes {\n411            let congruence: BigUint = &de % (prime - BigUint::one());\n412            if !congruence.is_one() {\n413                return Err(Error::InvalidExponent);\n414            }\n415        }\n416\n417        Ok(())\n418    }\n419\n420    /// Decrypt the given message.\n421    pub fn decrypt<P: PaddingScheme>(&self, padding: P, ciphertext: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {\n422        padding.decrypt(Option::<&mut DummyRng>::None, self, ciphertext)\n423    }\n424\n425    /// Decrypt the given message.\n426    ///\n427    /// Uses `rng` to blind the decryption process.\n428    pub fn decrypt_blinded<R: CryptoRngCore, P: PaddingScheme>(\n429        &self,\n430        rng: &mut R,\n431        padding: P,\n432        ciphertext: &[u8],\n433    ) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {\n434        padding.decrypt(Some(rng), self, ciphertext)\n435    }\n436\n437    /// Sign the given digest.\n438    pub fn sign<S: SignatureScheme>(&self, padding: S, digest_in: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {\n439        padding.sign(Option::<&mut DummyRng>::None, self, digest_in)\n440    }\n441\n442    /// Sign the given digest using the provided `rng`, which is used in the\n443    /// following ways depending on the [`SignatureScheme`]:\n444    ///\n445    /// - [`Pkcs1v15Sign`][`crate::Pkcs1v15Sign`] padding: uses the RNG\n446    ///   to mask the private key operation with random blinding, which helps\n447    ///   mitigate sidechannel attacks.\n448    /// - [`Pss`][`crate::Pss`] always requires randomness. Use\n449    ///   [`Pss::new`][`crate::Pss::new`] for a standard RSASSA-PSS signature, or\n450    ///   [`Pss::new_blinded`][`crate::Pss::new_blinded`] for RSA-BSSA blind\n451    ///   signatures.\n452    pub fn sign_with_rng<R: CryptoRngCore, S: SignatureScheme>(\n453        &self,\n454        rng: &mut R,\n455        padding: S,\n456        digest_in: &[u8],\n457    ) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {\n458        padding.sign(Some(rng), self, digest_in)\n459    }\n460}\n461\n462impl PrivateKeyParts for RsaPrivateKey {\n463    fn d(&self) -> &BigUint {\n464        &self.d\n465    }\n466\n467    fn primes(&self) -> &[BigUint] {\n468        &self.primes\n469    }\n470\n471    fn dp(&self) -> Option<&BigUint> {\n472        self.precomputed.as_ref().map(|p| &p.dp)\n473    }\n474\n475    fn dq(&self) -> Option<&BigUint> {\n476        self.precomputed.as_ref().map(|p| &p.dq)\n477    }\n478\n479    fn qinv(&self) -> Option<&BigInt> {\n480        self.precomputed.as_ref().map(|p| &p.qinv)\n481    }\n482\n483    fn crt_values(&self) -> Option<&[CrtValue]> {\n484        /* for some reason the standard self.precomputed.as_ref().map() doesn't work */\n485        if let Some(p) = &self.precomputed {\n486            Some(p.crt_values.as_slice())\n487        } else {\n488            None\n489        }\n490    }\n491}\n492\n493/// Check that the public key is well formed and has an exponent within acceptable bounds.\n494#[inline]\n495pub fn check_public(public_key: &impl PublicKeyParts) -> Result<()> {\n496    check_public_with_max_size(public_key, RsaPublicKey::MAX_SIZE)\n497}\n498\n499/// Check that the public key is well formed and has an exponent within acceptable bounds.\n500#[inline]\n501fn check_public_with_max_size(public_key: &impl PublicKeyParts, max_size: usize) -> Result<()> {\n502    if public_key.n().bits() > max_size {\n503        return Err(Error::ModulusTooLarge);\n504    }\n505\n506    let e = public_key\n507        .e()\n508        .to_u64()\n509        .ok_or(Error::PublicExponentTooLarge)?;\n510\n511    if public_key.e() >= public_key.n() || public_key.n().is_even() {\n512        return Err(Error::InvalidModulus);\n513    }\n514\n515    if public_key.e().is_even() {\n516        return Err(Error::InvalidExponent);\n517    }\n518\n519    if e < RsaPublicKey::MIN_PUB_EXPONENT {\n520        return Err(Error::PublicExponentTooSmall);\n521    }\n522\n523    if e > RsaPublicKey::MAX_PUB_EXPONENT {\n524        return Err(Error::PublicExponentTooLarge);\n525    }\n526\n527    Ok(())\n528}\n529\n530#[cfg(test)]\n531mod tests {\n532    use super::*;\n533    use crate::algorithms::rsa::{rsa_decrypt_and_check, \"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/RustCrypto/meta/master/logo_small.png\")]\n5#![warn(missing_docs)]\n6\n7//! # Supported algorithms\n8//!\n9//! This crate supports several schemes described in [RFC8017]:\n10//!\n11//! - [OAEP encryption scheme](#oaep-encryption)\n12//! - [PKCS#1 v1.5 encryption scheme](#pkcs1-v15-encryption)\n13//! - [PKCS#1 v1.5 signature scheme](#pkcs1-v15-signatures)\n14//! - [PSS signature scheme](#pss-signatures)\n15//!\n16//! These schemes are described below.\n17//!\n18//! # Usage\n19//!\n20//! ## OAEP encryption\n21//!\n22//! Note: requires `sha2` feature of `rsa` crate is enabled.\n23//!\n24#![cfg_attr(feature = \"sha2\", doc = \"```\")]\n25#![cfg_attr(not(feature = \"sha2\"), doc = \"```ignore\")]\n26//! use rsa::{RsaPrivateKey, RsaPublicKey, Oaep, sha2::Sha256};\n27//!\n28//! let mut rng = rand::thread_rng(); // rand@0.8\n29//!\n30//! let bits = 2048;\n31//! let private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\n32//! let public_key = RsaPublicKey::from(&private_key);\n33//!\n34//! // Encrypt\n35//! let data = b\"hello world\";\n36//! let padding = Oaep::new::<Sha256>();\n37//! let enc_data = public_key.encrypt(&mut rng, padding, &data[..]).expect(\"failed to encrypt\");\n38//! assert_ne!(&data[..], &enc_data[..]);\n39//!\n40//! // Decrypt\n41//! let padding = Oaep::new::<Sha256>();\n42//! let dec_data = private_key.decrypt(padding, &enc_data).expect(\"failed to decrypt\");\n43//! assert_eq!(&data[..], &dec_data[..]);\n44//! ```\n45//!\n46//! ## PKCS#1 v1.5 encryption\n47//! ```\n48//! use rsa::{RsaPrivateKey, RsaPublicKey, Pkcs1v15Encrypt};\n49//!\n50//! let mut rng = rand::thread_rng(); // rand@0.8\n51//!\n52//! let bits = 2048;\n53//! let private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\n54//! let public_key = RsaPublicKey::from(&private_key);\n55//!\n56//! // Encrypt\n57//! let data = b\"hello world\";\n58//! let enc_data = public_key.encrypt(&mut rng, Pkcs1v15Encrypt, &data[..]).expect(\"failed to encrypt\");\n59//! assert_ne!(&data[..], &enc_data[..]);\n60//!\n61//! // Decrypt\n62//! let dec_data = private_key.decrypt(Pkcs1v15Encrypt, &enc_data).expect(\"failed to decrypt\");\n63//! assert_eq!(&data[..], &dec_data[..]);\n64//! ```\n65//!\n66//! ## PKCS#1 v1.5 signatures\n67//!\n68//! Note: requires `sha2` feature of `rsa` crate is enabled.\n69//!\n70#![cfg_attr(feature = \"sha2\", doc = \"```\")]\n71#![cfg_attr(not(feature = \"sha2\"), doc = \"```ignore\")]\n72//! use rsa::RsaPrivateKey;\n73//! use rsa::pkcs1v15::{SigningKey, VerifyingKey};\n74//! use rsa::signature::{Keypair, RandomizedSigner, SignatureEncoding, Verifier};\n75//! use rsa::sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n76//!\n77//! let mut rng = rand::thread_rng(); // rand@0.8\n78//!\n79//! let bits = 2048;\n80//! let private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\n81//! let signing_key = SigningKey::<Sha256>::new(private_key);\n82//! let verifying_key = signing_key.verifying_key();\n83//!\n84//! // Sign\n85//! let data = b\"hello world\";\n86//! let signature = signing_key.sign_with_rng(&mut rng, data);\n87//! assert_ne!(signature.to_bytes().as_ref(), data.as_slice());\n88//!\n89//! // Verify\n90//! verifying_key.verify(data, &signature).expect(\"failed to verify\");\n91//! ```\n92//!\n93//! ## PSS signatures\n94//!\n95//! Note: requires `sha2` feature of `rsa` crate is enabled.\n96//!\n97#![cfg_attr(feature = \"sha2\", doc = \"```\")]\n98#![cfg_attr(not(feature = \"sha2\"), doc = \"```ignore\")]\n99//! use rsa::RsaPrivateKey;\n100//! use rsa::pss::{BlindedSigningKey, VerifyingKey};\n101//! use rsa::signature::{Keypair,RandomizedSigner, SignatureEncoding, Verifier};\n102//! use rsa::sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n103//!\n104//! let mut rng = rand::thread_rng(); // rand@0.8\n105//!\n106//! let bits = 2048;\n107//! let private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\n108//! let signing_key = BlindedSigningKey::<Sha256>::new(private_key);\n109//! let verifying_key = signing_key.verifying_key();\n110//!\n111//! // Sign\n112//! let data = b\"hello world\";\n113//! let signature = signing_key.sign_with_rng(&mut rng, data);\n114//! assert_ne!(signature.to_bytes().as_ref(), data);\n115//!\n116//! // Verify\n117//! verifying_key.verify(data, &signature).expect(\"failed to verify\");\n118//! ```\n119//!\n120//! ## PKCS#1 RSA Key Encoding\n121//!\n122//! PKCS#1 supports a legacy format for encoding RSA keys as binary (DER) or\n123//! text (PEM) data.\n124//!\n125//! You can recognize PEM encoded PKCS#1 keys because they have \"RSA * KEY\" in\n126//! the type label, e.g.:\n127//!\n128//! ```text\n129//! -----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----\n130//! ```\n131//!\n132//! Most modern applications use the newer PKCS#8 format instead (see below).\n133//!\n134//! The following traits can be used to decode/encode [`RsaPrivateKey`] and\n135//! [`RsaPublicKey`] as PKCS#1. Note that [`pkcs1`] is re-exported from the\n136//! toplevel of the `rsa` crate:\n137//!\n138//! - [`pkcs1::DecodeRsaPrivateKey`]: decode RSA private keys from PKCS#1\n139//! - [`pkcs1::EncodeRsaPrivateKey`]: encode RSA private keys to PKCS#1\n140//! - [`pkcs1::DecodeRsaPublicKey`]: decode RSA public keys from PKCS#1\n141//! - [`pkcs1::EncodeRsaPublicKey`]: encode RSA public keys to PKCS#1\n142//!\n143//! ### Example\n144//!\n145//! ```\n146//! # fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n147//! # #[cfg(all(feature = \"pem\", feature = \"std\"))]\n148//! # {\n149//! use rsa::{RsaPublicKey, pkcs1::DecodeRsaPublicKey};\n150//!\n151//! let pem = \"-----BEGIN RSA PUBLIC KEY-----\n152//! MIIBCgKCAQEAtsQsUV8QpqrygsY+2+JCQ6Fw8/omM71IM2N/R8pPbzbgOl0p78MZ\n153//! GsgPOQ2HSznjD0FPzsH8oO2B5Uftws04LHb2HJAYlz25+lN5cqfHAfa3fgmC38Ff\n154//! wBkn7l582UtPWZ/wcBOnyCgb3yLcvJrXyrt8QxHJgvWO23ITrUVYszImbXQ67YGS\n155//! 0YhMrbixRzmo2tpm3JcIBtnHrEUMsT0NfFdfsZhTT8YbxBvA8FdODgEwx7u/vf3J\n156//! 9qbi4+Kv8cvqyJuleIRSjVXPsIMnoejIn04APPKIjpMyQdnWlby7rNyQtE4+CV+j\n157//! cFjqJbE/Xilcvqxt6DirjFCvYeKYl1uHLwIDAQAB\n158//! -----END RSA PUBLIC KEY-----\";\n159//!\n160//! let public_key = RsaPublicKey::from_pkcs1_pem(pem)?;\n161//! # }\n162//! # Ok(())\n163//! # }\n164//! ```\n165//!\n166//! ## PKCS#8 RSA Key Encoding\n167//!\n168//! PKCS#8 is a private key format with support for multiple algorithms.\n169//! Like PKCS#1, it can be encoded as binary (DER) or text (PEM).\n170//!\n171//! You can recognize PEM encoded PKCS#8 keys because they *don't* have\n172//! an algorithm name in the type label, e.g.:\n173//!\n174//! ```text\n175//! -----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----\n176//! ```\n177//!\n178//! The following traits can be used to decode/encode [`RsaPrivateKey`] and\n179//! [`RsaPublicKey`] as PKCS#8. no_std)]\n#![cfg_attr(docsrs, feature(doc_auto_cfg))]\n#![doc = include_str!(\"../README.md\")]\n#![doc(html_logo_url = \"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/RustCrypto/meta/master/logo_small.png\")]\n#![warn(missing_docs)]\n\n//! # Supported algorithms\n//!\n//! This crate supports several schemes described in [RFC8017]:\n//!\n//! - [OAEP encryption scheme](#oaep-encryption)\n//! - [PKCS#1 v1.5 encryption scheme](#pkcs1-v15-encryption)\n//! - [PKCS#1 v1.5 signature scheme](#pkcs1-v15-signatures)\n//! - [PSS signature scheme](#pss-signatures)\n//!\n//! These schemes are described below.\n//!\n//! # Usage\n//!\n//! ## OAEP encryption\n//! ```\n//! use rsa::{RsaPrivateKey, RsaPublicKey, Oaep};\n//!\n//! let mut rng = rand::thread_rng();\n//!\n//! let bits = 2048;\n//! let private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\n//! let public_key = RsaPublicKey::from(&private_key);\n//!\n//! // Encrypt\n//! let data = b\"hello world\";\n//! let padding = Oaep::new::<sha2::Sha256>();\n//! let enc_data = public_key.encrypt(&mut rng, padding, &data[..]).expect(\"failed to encrypt\");\n//! assert_ne!(&data[..], &enc_data[..]);\n//!\n//! // Decrypt\n//! let padding = Oaep::new::<sha2::Sha256>();\n//! let dec_data = private_key.decrypt(padding, &enc_data).expect(\"failed to decrypt\");\n//! assert_eq!(&data[..], &dec_data[..]);\n//! ```\n//!\n//! ## PKCS#1 v1.5 encryption\n//! ```\n//! use rsa::{RsaPrivateKey, RsaPublicKey, Pkcs1v15Encrypt};\n//!\n//! let mut rng = rand::thread_rng();\n//!\n//! let bits = 2048;\n//! let private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\n//! let public_key = RsaPublicKey::from(&private_key);\n//!\n//! // Encrypt\n//! let data = b\"hello world\";\n//! let enc_data = public_key.encrypt(&mut rng, Pkcs1v15Encrypt, &data[..]).expect(\"failed to encrypt\");\n//! assert_ne!(&data[..], &enc_data[..]);\n//!\n//! // Decrypt\n//! let dec_data = private_key.decrypt(Pkcs1v15Encrypt, &enc_data).expect(\"failed to decrypt\");\n//! assert_eq!(&data[..], &dec_data[..]);\n//! ```\n//!\n//! ## PKCS#1 v1.5 signatures\n//!\n//! Note: requires `sha2` feature of `rsa` crate is enabled.\n//!\n#![cfg_attr(feature = \"sha2\", doc = \"```\")]\n#![cfg_attr(not(feature = \"sha2\"), doc = \"```ignore\")]\n//! use rsa::RsaPrivateKey;\n//! use rsa::pkcs1v15::{SigningKey, VerifyingKey};\n//! use rsa::signature::{Keypair, RandomizedSigner, SignatureEncoding, Verifier};\n//! use rsa::sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n//!\n//! let mut rng = rand::thread_rng();\n//!\n//! let bits = 2048;\n//! let private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\n//! let signing_key = SigningKey::<Sha256>::new(private_key);\n//! let verifying_key = signing_key.verifying_key();\n//!\n//! // Sign\n//! let data = b\"hello world\";\n//! let signature = signing_key.sign_with_rng(&mut rng, data);\n//! assert_ne!(signature.to_bytes().as_ref(), data.as_slice());\n//!\n//! // Verify\n//! verifying_key.verify(data, &signature).expect(\"failed to verify\");\n//! ```\n//!\n//! ## PSS signatures\n//!\n//! Note: requires `sha2` feature of `rsa` crate is enabled.\n//!\n#![cfg_attr(feature = \"sha2\", doc = \"```\")]\n#![cfg_attr(not(feature = \"sha2\"), doc = \"```ignore\")]\n//! use rsa::RsaPrivateKey;\n//! use rsa::pss::{BlindedSigningKey, VerifyingKey};\n//! use rsa::signature::{Keypair,RandomizedSigner, SignatureEncoding, Verifier};\n//! use rsa::sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n//!\n//! let mut rng = rand::thread_rng();\n//!\n//! let bits = 2048;\n//! let private_key = RsaPrivateKey::new(&mut rng, bits).expect(\"failed to generate a key\");\n//! let signing_key = BlindedSigningKey::<Sha256>::new(private_key);\n//! let verifying_key = signing_key.verifying_key();\n//!\n//! // Sign\n//! let data = b\"hello world\";\n//! let signature = signing_key.sign_with_rng(&mut rng, data);\n//! assert_ne!(signature.to_bytes().as_ref(), data);\n//!\n//! // Verify\n//! verifying_key.verify(data, &signature).expect(\"failed to verify\");\n//! ```\n//!\n//! ## PKCS#1 RSA Key Encoding\n//!\n//! PKCS#1 supports a legacy format for encoding RSA keys as binary (DER) or\n//! text (PEM) data.\n//!\n//! You can recognize PEM encoded PKCS#1 keys because they have \"RSA * KEY\" in\n//! the type label, e.g.:\n//!\n//! ```text\n//! -----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----\n//! ```\n//!\n//! Most modern applications use the newer PKCS#8 format instead (see below).\n//!\n//! The following traits can be used to decode/encode [`RsaPrivateKey`] and\n//! [`RsaPublicKey`] as PKCS#1. Note that [`pkcs1`] is re-exported from the\n//! toplevel of the `rsa` crate:\n//!\n//! - [`pkcs1::DecodeRsaPrivateKey`]: decode RSA private keys from PKCS#1\n//! - [`pkcs1::EncodeRsaPrivateKey`]: encode RSA private keys to PKCS#1\n//! - [`pkcs1::DecodeRsaPublicKey`]: decode RSA public keys from PKCS#1\n//! - [`pkcs1::EncodeRsaPublicKey`]: encode RSA public keys to PKCS#1\n//!\n//! ### Example\n//!\n//! ```\n//! # fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n//! # #[cfg(all(feature = \"pem\", feature = \"std\"))]\n//! # {\n//! use rsa::{RsaPublicKey, pkcs1::DecodeRsaPublicKey};\n//!\n//! let pem = \"-----BEGIN RSA PUBLIC KEY-----\n//! MIIBCgKCAQEAtsQsUV8QpqrygsY+2+JCQ6Fw8/omM71IM2N/R8pPbzbgOl0p78MZ\n//! GsgPOQ2HSznjD0FPzsH8oO2B5Uftws04LHb2HJAYlz25+lN5cqfHAfa3fgmC38Ff\n//! wBkn7l582UtPWZ/wcBOnyCgb3yLcvJrXyrt8QxHJgvWO23ITrUVYszImbXQ67YGS\n//! 0YhMrbixRzmo2tpm3JcIBtnHrEUMsT0NfFdfsZhTT8YbxBvA8FdODgEwx7u/vf3J\n//! 9qbi4+Kv8cvqyJuleIRSjVXPsIMnoejIn04APPKIjpMyQdnWlby7rNyQtE4+CV+j\n//! cFjqJbE/Xilcvqxt6DirjFCvYeKYl1uHLwIDAQAB\n//! -----END RSA PUBLIC KEY-----\";\n//!\n//! let public_key = RsaPublicKey::from_pkcs1_pem(pem)?;\n//! # }\n//! # Ok(())\n//! # }\n//! ```\n//!\n//! ## PKCS#8 RSA Key Encoding\n//!\n//! PKCS#8 is a private key format with support for multiple algorithms.\n//! Like PKCS#1, it can be encoded as binary (DER) or text (PEM).\n//!\n//! You can recognize PEM encoded PKCS#8 keys because they *don't* have\n//! an algorithm name in the type label, e.g.:\n//!\n//! ```text\n//! -----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----\n//! ```\n//!\n//! The following traits can be used to decode/encode [`RsaPrivateKey`] and\n//! [`RsaPublicKey`] as PKCS#8. Note that [`pkcs8`] is re-exported from the\n//! toplevel of the `rsa` crate:\n//!\n//! - [`pkcs8::DecodePrivateKey`]: decode private keys from PKCS#8\n//! - [`pkcs8::EncodePrivateKey`]: encode private keys to PKCS#8\n//! - [`pkcs8::DecodePublicKey`]: decode public keys from PKCS#8\n//! - [`pkcs8::EncodePublicKey`]: encode public keys to PKCS#8\n//!\n//! ### Example\n//!\n//! ```\n//! # fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n//! # #[cfg(all(feature = \"pem\", feature = \"std\"))]\n//! # {\n//! use rsa::{RsaPublicKey, pkcs8::DecodePublicKey};\n//!\n//! let pem = \"-----BEGIN PUBLIC KEY-----\n//! MIIBIjANBgkqhkiG9w0BAQEFAAOCAQ8AMIIBCgKCAQEAtsQsUV8QpqrygsY+2+JC\n//! Q6Fw8/omM71IM2N/R8pPbzbgOl0p78MZGsgPOQ2HSznjD0FPzsH8oO2B5Uftws04\n//! LHb2HJAYlz25+lN5cqfHAfa3fgmC38FfwBkn7l582UtPWZ/wcBOnyCgb3yLcvJrX\n//! yrt8QxHJgvWO23ITrUVYszImbXQ67YGS0YhMrbixRzmo2tpm3JcIBtnHrEUMsT0N\n//! fFdfsZhTT8YbxBvA8FdODgEwx7u/vf3J9qbi4+Kv8cvqyJuleIRSjVXPsIMnoejI\n//! n04APPKIjpMyQdnWlby7rNyQtE4+CV+jcFjqJbE/Xilcvqxt6DirjFCvYeKYl1uH\n//!
